Task CS-01

population controlled family CS-01 source https://github.com/python-humanize/humanize @ a95004512dde · status pending_review

User turns in the source session

CS-01-session/L000001.00 scripted_user Hey - in humanize, `naturalsize` always prints one decimal place, so 3,000,000 bytes shows as "3.0 MB" and 1024 bytes with binary=True shows as "1.0 KiB". I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB" and "1 KiB", but keep the decimal when it's meaningful (e.g. "2.9 KiB" should stay as it is). Can you implement that? The repo is checked out (as a git working copy) at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/humanize. Code from the repo can't be run directly on this machine; use the two wrapper scripts, which run it in an isolated container: /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/run_tests.sh [pytest args] (e.g. run_tests.sh -q tests/test_filesize.py) and /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/run_python.sh -c '...'. Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01.
CS-01-session/L000125.00 scripted_user The coordinator sent a message while you were working: Thanks. Actually, I just realized that changing the default output is going to break a bunch of our templates and snapshot tests that expect "3.0 MB", and naturalsize is supposed to stay compatible with jinja2's filesizeformat. Please keep the default output exactly as it was before, and make the trailing-".0" trimming opt-in through a new keyword argument `strip_trailing_zero` (default False). Existing calls that pass a custom `format` string, including positional ones like naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f"), must keep producing exactly what they produced before. Address this before completing your current task.

VariantRequirementKindStatusStatementEvidence
initial_onlyR1changeexplicitIn the default (decimal) mode naturalsize no longer prints a trailing ".0" for a whole-number value: naturalsize(3000000) returns "3 MB" instead of "3.0 MB".“so 3,000,000 bytes shows as "3.0 MB"”; “I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB"”
initial_onlyR2changeexplicitIn binary mode naturalsize no longer prints a trailing ".0" for a whole-number value: naturalsize(1024, binary=True) returns "1 KiB" instead of "1.0 KiB".“1024 bytes with binary=True shows as "1.0 KiB"”; “drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB" and "1 KiB"”
initial_onlyR3invariantexplicitWhen the one-decimal number has a non-zero decimal digit, naturalsize output is unchanged and keeps its single decimal place: "2.9 KiB" (e.g. naturalsize(3000, binary=True)) stays exactly "2.9 KiB" - it is neither rounded to a whole number nor given extra digits.“but keep the decimal when it's meaningful (e.g. "2.9 KiB" should stay as it is)”
initial_onlyR4changeexplicitThe trailing-".0" removal is general rather than limited to the two quoted inputs: any naturalsize result (default format) whose number would print as N.0 is printed as N, for every unit in both decimal and binary modes, without touching zeros of the integer part.“`naturalsize` always prints one decimal place”; “I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0"”
initial_onlyR5changeinferredThe decision is based on the printed (rounded) one-decimal value, not on the raw value being an exact integer: a size that rounds to a whole number at one decimal place also loses the ".0", e.g. naturalsize(1040000) returns "1 MB" (currently "1.0 MB") and naturalsize(2999999) returns "3 MB" (currently "3.0 MB").“I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0"”; “keep the decimal when it's meaningful”
initial_onlyR6changeunresolvedUnresolved: the session does not say whether the trailing-".0" removal also applies to GNU-style output (gnu=True), i.e. whether naturalsize(1024, gnu=True) should stay "1.0K" or become "1K".“`naturalsize` always prints one decimal place”; “I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0"”

initial_onlyR9constraintexplicitProcess constraint: all work stays inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01, with the code change made in the humanize git working copy at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/humanize.“Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01.”; “The repo is checked out (as a git working copy) at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/humanize.”
full_sessionR1changesupersededBy default (no extra arguments), naturalsize drops a trailing ".0" from the formatted number: naturalsize(3000000) returns "3 MB" instead of "3.0 MB", and naturalsize(1024, binary=True) returns "1 KiB" instead of "1.0 KiB".“I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB" and "1 KiB"”; “so 3,000,000 bytes shows as "3.0 MB" and 1024 bytes with binary=True shows as "1.0 KiB"”
full_sessionR2invariantexplicitThe trailing-".0" trimming must keep a meaningful (non-zero) decimal: 3000 bytes with binary=True still renders as "2.9 KiB" both by default and with trimming enabled, i.e. naturalsize(3000, binary=True) == "2.9 KiB" and naturalsize(3000, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "2.9 KiB".“but keep the decimal when it's meaningful (e.g. "2.9 KiB" should stay as it is)”
full_sessionR3constraintexplicitAll work stays inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01, with the change made in the git working copy /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/humanize, and repository code is never run directly on the host: tests and Python snippets run only through the wrappers /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/run_tests.sh and /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01/run_python.sh, which use an isolated container.“Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-01.”; “Code from the repo can't be run directly on this machine; use the two wrapper scripts”
full_sessionR4invariantexplicitThe default output of naturalsize (strip_trailing_zero not passed, default format) stays exactly what the base version produced, for every input and mode, e.g. naturalsize(3000000) == "3.0 MB", naturalsize(1024, binary=True) == "1.0 KiB", naturalsize(3000, binary=True) == "2.9 KiB"; in particular non-GNU output stays compatible with jinja2's filesizeformat filter (one decimal place, e.g. "3.0 kB" for 3000) wherever the base version matched it.“changing the default output is going to break a bunch of our templates and snapshot tests that expect "3.0 MB"”; “Please keep the default output exactly as it was before”; “naturalsize is supposed to stay compatible with jinja2's filesizeformat”
full_sessionR5changeexplicitnaturalsize gains a new keyword argument named strip_trailing_zero, default False, that makes the trailing-".0" trimming opt-in: naturalsize(3000000, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "3 MB", naturalsize(1024, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "1 KiB", naturalsize(3000, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "2.9 KiB"; omitting it or passing strip_trailing_zero=False gives the unchanged default output (naturalsize(3000000, strip_trailing_zero=False) == "3.0 MB").“make the trailing-".0" trimming opt-in through a new keyword argument `strip_trailing_zero` (default False)”
full_sessionR6invariantexplicitCalls that pass a custom format string, positionally or by keyword (and without strip_trailing_zero), return exactly what they returned before; the positional parameter order (value, binary, gnu, format) is unchanged, so naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f") still returns "2.930K" and naturalsize(3000000, format="%.2f") still returns "3.00 MB".“Existing calls that pass a custom `format` string, including positional ones like naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f")”; “must keep producing exactly what they produced before”
full_sessionR7changeunresolvedHow strip_trailing_zero=True combines with a custom format is not specified: e.g. whether naturalsize(3000000, format="%.2f", strip_trailing_zero=True) should return "3 MB" or "3.00 MB", and whether naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f", strip_trailing_zero=True) should return "2.93K" or "2.930K".“pass a custom `format` string, including positional ones”; “trimming opt-in through a new keyword argument `strip_trailing_zero`”
full_sessionR8changeinferredThe trailing-".0" trimming (strip_trailing_zero=True) works the same way in every mode and for negative sizes: naturalsize(1024, gnu=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "1K" and naturalsize(3000, gnu=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "2.9K" (while naturalsize(1024, gnu=True) stays "1.0K"), naturalsize(-3000000, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "-3 MB", naturalsize(-4096, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "-4 KiB", naturalsize(-3000, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "-2.9 KiB".“Hey - in humanize, `naturalsize` always prints one decimal place”
full_sessionR9constraintinferredWith strip_trailing_zero=True the decision is made on the rendered one-decimal number and only a ".0" after the decimal point is removed: values that round to X.0 lose it (naturalsize(3000001, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "3 MB", naturalsize(999999, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "1 MB"), integer digits are kept (naturalsize(10**7, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "10 MB", not "1 MB"; naturalsize(10**8, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "100 MB"; naturalsize(3 * 10**34, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "30000 QB"), and outputs without a decimal part are unchanged (naturalsize(100, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "100 Bytes", naturalsize(1, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "1 Byte").“drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB"”; “keep the decimal when it's meaningful”
referenceR-default-trimchangesupersedednaturalsize drops a trailing '.0' by default (3000000 -> '3 MB'; 1024 with binary=True -> '1 KiB').“I'd like it to drop a trailing ".0" so those read "3 MB" and "1 KiB"”
referenceR-keep-meaningfulinvariantexplicitTrailing-zero trimming never removes a meaningful decimal: naturalsize(3000, binary=True) == '2.9 KiB' and naturalsize(3000, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == '2.9 KiB'.“keep the decimal when it's meaningful (e.g. "2.9 KiB" should stay as it is)”
referenceR-opt-inchangeexplicitnaturalsize accepts a keyword argument strip_trailing_zero (default False); with strip_trailing_zero=True a trailing '.0' is dropped: naturalsize(3000000, strip_trailing_zero=True) == '3 MB' and naturalsize(1024, binary=True, strip_trailing_zero=True) == '1 KiB'.“make the trailing-".0" trimming opt-in through a new keyword argument `strip_trailing_zero` (default False)”; “so those read "3 MB" and "1 KiB"”
referenceR-default-unchangedinvariantexplicitWithout strip_trailing_zero, naturalsize output is exactly unchanged for all inputs, e.g. naturalsize(3000000) == '3.0 MB', naturalsize(1024, binary=True) == '1.0 KiB', naturalsize(3000, gnu=True) == '2.9K', naturalsize(300) == '300 Bytes', naturalsize(1) == '1 Byte'.“Please keep the default output exactly as it was before”
referenceR-custom-format-unchangedinvariantexplicitCalls passing a custom format (as keyword or as the 4th positional argument) produce exactly the previous output, e.g. naturalsize(3000, False, True, '%.3f') == '2.930K' and naturalsize(3000000, format='%.2f') == '3.00 MB'.“Existing calls that pass a custom `format` string, including positional ones like naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f"), must keep producing exactly what they produced before.”

+@pytest.mark.parametrize(
+    "test_args, expected",
+    [
+        ([3000000], "3 MB"),
+        (["1000"], "1 kB"),
+        ([10**3], "1 kB"),
+        ([999999], "1 MB"),
+        ([1024, True], "1 KiB"),
+        ([1024, False, True], "1K"),
+        # Meaningful decimals and integer zeros are never removed.
+        ([3000, True], "2.9 KiB"),
+        ([3000, False, True], "2.9K"),
+        ([10**28], "10 RB"),
+        ([10**34 * 3], "30000 QB"),
+        # Byte counts have no decimal part to strip.
+        ([300], "300 Bytes"),
+        ([300, False, True], "300B"),
+        ([1], "1 Byte"),
+        # Custom formats: only insignificant zeros after the decimal point go.
+        ([3000000, False, False, "%.2f"], "3 MB"),
+        ([2500000, False, False, "%.2f"], "2.5 MB"),
+        ([3141592, False, False, "%.2f"], "3.14 MB"),
+        ([3000, False, True, "%.3f"], "2.93K"),
+        ([3000000000, False, True, "%.0f"], "3G"),
+        ([10**5, False, False, "%.0f"], "100 kB"),
+    ],
+)
+def test_naturalsize_strip_trailing_zero(
+    test_args: list[int | bool | str], expected: str
+) -> None:
+    assert humanize.naturalsize(*test_args, strip_trailing_zero=True) == expected
+    # Stripping is opt-in: False behaves exactly like not passing the argument.
+    assert humanize.naturalsize(
+        *test_args, strip_trailing_zero=False
+    ) == humanize.naturalsize(*test_args)
+
+    # Retest with negative input
+    if isinstance(test_args[0], int):
+        test_args[0] *= -1
+    else:
+        test_args[0] = f"-{test_args[0]}"
+
+    assert humanize.naturalsize(*test_args, strip_trailing_zero=True) == "-" + expected

+def _strip_trailing_zeros(number: str) -> str:
+    """Remove insignificant zeros from the decimal fraction that ends `number`.
+
+    The decimal point is dropped as well once no digits remain after it, so "3.0"
+    becomes "3", "2.50" becomes "2.5" and "2.9" stays "2.9". Only a non-empty run
+    of digits at the very end of the string, following a decimal point that itself
+    follows a digit, counts as a fraction. Integer digits ("20"), exponents
+    ("2.0e+01") and literal text from a custom format are left untouched, so a
+    meaningful digit is never removed.
+
+    Args:
+        number (str): The scaled value as rendered by the `format` string.
+
+    Returns:
+        str: `number` without its insignificant trailing zeros.
+    """
+    whole, point, fraction = number.rpartition(".")
+    if not (
+        point
+        and fraction
+        and _ASCII_DIGITS.issuperset(fraction)
+        and whole[-1:] in _ASCII_DIGITS
+    ):
+        return number
+    fraction = fraction.rstrip("0")
+    return f"{whole}.{fraction}" if fraction else whole
+

+@pytest.mark.parametrize("binary, gnu", [(False, False), (True, False), (False, True)])
+def test_naturalsize_strip_trailing_zero_only_drops_point_zero(
+    binary: bool, gnu: bool
+) -> None:
+    # With the default format, the only difference stripping can make is that a
+    # ".0" right after the integer digits disappears; everything else is identical.
+    point_zero = re.compile(r"^(-?\d+)\.0(?=\D)")
+    for exponent in range(36):
+        magnitudes = (10**exponent, 10**exponent - 1, 3 * 10**exponent, 2**exponent)
+        for value in (*magnitudes, *(-magnitude for magnitude in magnitudes)):
+            plain = humanize.naturalsize(value, binary, gnu)
+            stripped = humanize.naturalsize(
+                value, binary, gnu, strip_trailing_zero=True
+            )
+            assert stripped == point_zero.sub(r"\1", plain), value
+
+
+def test_naturalsize_strip_trailing_zero_is_keyword_only() -> None:
+    # `format` stays the 4th positional argument, so existing positional calls keep
+    # their meaning, and the new flag can only be given by keyword.
+    assert humanize.naturalsize(3000, False, True, "%.3f") == "2.930K"
+    too_many: list[typing.Any] = [3000, False, True, "%.3f", True]
+    with pytest.raises(TypeError):
+        humanize.naturalsize(*too_many)

Task CS-02
population controlled family CS-02 source https://github.com/msiemens/tinydb @ edff96aeb261 · status pending_review


User turns in the source session


CS-02-session/L000001.00 scripted_user
TinyDB's JSONStorage rewrites the database file in place (seek to 0, write, truncate), so if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write, the JSON file is left half-written and the whole database becomes unreadable. Can you make JSONStorage writes atomic - write the new contents to a temporary file and then swap it into place - so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one?

The repo is checked out (as a git working copy) at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/tinydb. Code from the repo can't be run directly on this machine; use the two wrapper scripts, which run it in an isolated container: /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/run_tests.sh [pytest args] (e.g. run_tests.sh -q tests) and /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/run_python.sh -c '...'. Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02.


CS-02-session/L000160.00 scripted_user
The coordinator sent a message while you were working:
Thanks! A few things I should have said up front: our app keeps a single TinyDB instance open for its whole lifetime and reads right after writing, so reads through the same instance (and the same storage object) must always see what was just written. Please also keep honoring the `encoding` argument and the extra json kwargs (like indent=4 or sort_keys=True) exactly as before, so the bytes written are the same as today. And don't leave temporary files lying around next to the database file - not after a normal write, and not when a write fails halfway (in that case the original file must stay exactly as it was).

Address this before completing your current task.


Contracts
VariantRequirementKindStatusStatementEvidence


initial_onlyR1changeexplicitJSONStorage.write no longer rewrites the database file in place (seek to 0, write, truncate): it writes the complete new serialized contents to a temporary file and then swaps that file into place over the database path with an atomic rename/replace (e.g. os.replace). After a successful write the database file contains exactly the new serialized data (also when the new contents are shorter than the old ones).“Can you make JSONStorage writes atomic - write the new contents to a temporary file and then swap it into place”; “TinyDB's JSONStorage rewrites the database file in place (seek to 0, write, truncate)”


initial_onlyR2invariantexplicitThe database file on disk is always either the complete old version or the complete new version, never half-written: if the process dies or the write fails part-way (e.g. the disk fills up, an OSError such as ENOSPC/EFBIG while the new contents are being written, or any other error before the swap completes), the file at the database path is byte-for-byte the previous version and still loads with its previous data.“so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”; “if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write, the JSON file is left half-written”


initial_onlyR3invariantinferredAfter a write has been swapped into place, the same JSONStorage/TinyDB instance keeps working on the new file: subsequent reads through that instance return the newly written data (not the stale contents of the replaced file), and successive writes build on each other without losing earlier updates. A TinyDB opened afterwards on the same path also sees the latest data.“the JSON file is left half-written and the whole database becomes unreadable”; “so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”


initial_onlyR4invariantinferredApart from how the file is written, existing JSONStorage/TinyDB behaviour is preserved: every option the base JSONStorage supports keeps working as before (e.g. create_dirs, encoding, access_mode, extra json.dumps keyword arguments such as indent/sort_keys), the text written to the file is the same as the base implementation produces (same json.dumps arguments and encoding), a storage opened read-only (where the base supports access_mode='r') still refuses writes with an IOError/OSError without modifying the database file, relative database paths such as TinyDB('db.json') work, and the repository's existing test suite still passes.“Can you make JSONStorage writes atomic”; “run_tests.sh [pytest args] (e.g. run_tests.sh -q tests)”


initial_onlyR5constraintinferredThe temporary file is created on the same filesystem as the database file (e.g. in the database file's directory), so the swap is a single atomic rename that works regardless of where the system temp directory is; the write must not degrade into a non-atomic copy over the database file.“write the new contents to a temporary file and then swap it into place”; “so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”


initial_onlyR6invariantinferredWrites do not leave temporary files behind: after a successful write the temporary file has become the database file, and when a write fails with an exception (e.g. disk full while writing the temporary file, or an error during the swap) the partially written temporary file is removed, so the database's directory contains the same files as before the write.“write the new contents to a temporary file”; “if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write”


initial_onlyR7invariantinferredA write that cannot be completed is reported to the caller: if writing or swapping the temporary file fails, JSONStorage.write (and therefore the TinyDB operation that triggered it, e.g. insert/update) raises an exception instead of returning normally as if the data had been saved.“the disk fills up in the middle of a write”


initial_onlyR8invariantinferredThe new contents are completely written, flushed and fsync'ed (os.fsync or os.fdatasync) to the temporary file before it is swapped into place, so the swap never installs a file whose data has not reached the disk, and errors that only surface on flush/fsync (e.g. a full disk) abort the write before the database file is replaced.“so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”; “if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write”


initial_onlyR9constraintexplicitRepository code is never run directly on the host machine; tests and Python snippets are run only through the provided wrappers /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/run_tests.sh [pytest args] and /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/run_python.sh -c '...', which execute the code in an isolated container.“Code from the repo can't be run directly on this machine; use the two wrapper scripts, which run it in an isolated container”


initial_onlyR10constraintexplicitAll work stays inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02: the change is made in the git working copy of TinyDB at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/tinydb, and no files outside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02 are created or modified.“Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02.”; “The repo is checked out (as a git working copy) at /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02/tinydb.”


full_sessionR1changeexplicitJSONStorage writes must be atomic: at every point in time, including when the writing process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write, the database file on disk is a complete, readable JSON file holding either the full old data or the full new data. It must never be left half-written or corrupted. For example, an interrupted write of a smaller new state over a larger old state must not leave the new prefix followed by leftover old bytes.“Can you make JSONStorage writes atomic”; “so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”; “if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write”; “the JSON file is left half-written and the whole database becomes unreadable”


full_sessionR2constraintexplicitThe atomic write must work by writing the complete new contents to a separate temporary file and then swapping that file into place over the database path (e.g. os.replace or os.rename). It must not rewrite the existing database file in place with seek(0)/write/truncate.“write the new contents to a temporary file and then swap it into place”; “rewrites the database file in place (seek to 0, write, truncate)”


full_sessionR3invariantexplicitReads through the same TinyDB instance and the same JSONStorage object must always return what was just written, for the whole lifetime of a single long-lived instance. After storage.write(X), storage.read() == X immediately, for every write in a sequence (write A, read -> A; write B, read -> B). Through TinyDB, after db.insert({'a': 1}) on an instance kept open, db.all() and db.storage.read() immediately include the new document.“our app keeps a single TinyDB instance open for its whole lifetime and reads right after writing”; “reads through the same instance (and the same storage object) must always see what was just written”


full_sessionR4invariantexplicitThe encoding argument of JSONStorage must keep being honored exactly as before. For any encoding (default/None, 'utf-8', 'utf-8-sig', 'utf-16', 'latin-1', ...), the bytes written to the database file must be identical to those the original in-place implementation writes for the same data and kwargs, on the first and on later writes. For an explicit codec that is json.dumps(data, **kwargs).encode(encoding), with a single BOM at the start for codecs like 'utf-16'/'utf-8-sig'. The data must also read back correctly with that encoding.“Please also keep honoring the `encoding` argument and the extra json kwargs”; “so the bytes written are the same as today”


full_sessionR5invariantexplicitExtra keyword arguments given to JSONStorage (e.g. through TinyDB(path, indent=4, sort_keys=True)) must keep being forwarded to json.dumps exactly as before. The database file bytes must be identical to what the original implementation writes for the same data and kwargs (e.g. indent=4, sort_keys=True, separators, ensure_ascii=False).“the extra json kwargs (like indent=4 or sort_keys=True) exactly as before”; “so the bytes written are the same as today”


full_sessionR6constraintexplicitAfter a normal (successful) write, no temporary files are left next to the database file. The directory containing the database holds exactly the same set of files as before the write (e.g. only 'db.json').“leave temporary files lying around next to the database file”; “not after a normal write”


full_sessionR7constraintexplicitWhen a write fails halfway, no temporary file is left next to the database file. A halfway failure is an exception during the write, e.g. OSError(ENOSPC) while writing, flushing or fsyncing the new data, a failure of the rename/replace step, or a value json cannot serialize. After the failed write, the database directory contains exactly the files it contained before.“leave temporary files lying around next to the database file”; “and not when a write fails halfway”


full_sessionR8invariantexplicitWhen a write fails halfway, the original database file must stay exactly as it was. Its contents must be byte-for-byte identical (not truncated, partially overwritten or replaced), so the database still loads with its previous data.“and not when a write fails halfway”; “in that case the original file must stay exactly as it was”


full_sessionR9invariantinferredAfter a failed write, the same long-lived JSONStorage/TinyDB instance must remain usable. storage.read() returns the original (pre-failure) data, and a later successful write is persisted and immediately visible to reads through the same instance.“our app keeps a single TinyDB instance open for its whole lifetime”; “in that case the original file must stay exactly as it was”


full_sessionR10invariantinferredRepeated writes on a single long-lived JSONStorage must not leak open file handles. The process's number of open file descriptors stays bounded instead of growing with the number of writes, so the instance keeps working over thousands of write/read cycles.“our app keeps a single TinyDB instance open for its whole lifetime”; “must always see what was just written”


full_sessionR11constraintunresolvedUnresolved: the session does not settle whether a temporary file left behind when the writing process is killed mid-write must be removed later, e.g. the next time the database is opened. The killed process cannot clean up after itself. The user forbids leftover temporary files after a normal write and when a write fails halfway, but never says whether a killed process counts as a write that fails halfway.“and not when a write fails halfway”; “if the process dies or the disk fills up in the middle of a write”


full_sessionR12constraintexplicitProcess constraint: all work must stay inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02 (the tinydb working copy), and repository code is run only through the provided wrapper scripts run_tests.sh and run_python.sh (isolated container).“Please keep all your work inside /home/user/cs_workspaces/CS-02”; “use the two wrapper scripts, which run it in an isolated container”


referenceR-atomicchangeexplicitIf a write fails partway (e.g. an OS-level write error such as exceeding the file-size limit), the database file keeps exactly its previous contents and remains valid JSON; it is never left partially written.“so the file on disk is always either the old version or the new one”


referenceR-new-contentinvariantexplicitAfter a successful write the database file contains exactly the newly written data.“either the old version or the new one”


referenceR-read-after-writeinvariantexplicitReads through the same TinyDB instance / JSONStorage object after one or more writes return the data just written; a new TinyDB opened on the same path also sees it.“reads through the same instance (and the same storage object) must always see what was just written”


referenceR-bytes-unchangedinvariantexplicitFor a given encoding and json kwargs (e.g. indent=4, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False), the bytes written are exactly json.dumps(data, **kwargs) encoded with that encoding, as at the base commit.“keep honoring the `encoding` argument and the extra json kwargs (like indent=4 or sort_keys=True) exactly as before, so the bytes written are the same as today”


referenceR-no-temp-filesconstraintexplicitNo temporary files remain in the database directory after successful writes or after a failed write.“don't leave temporary files lying around next to the database file - not after a normal write, and not when a write fails halfway”


referenceR-original-intactinvariantexplicitWhen a write fails halfway, the original file stays byte-for-byte as it was.“in that case the original file must stay exactly as it was”

+def test_json_write_replaces_contents(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    kwargs = {'ensure_ascii': False, 'sort_keys': True}
+    storage = JSONStorage(path, encoding='utf-8', **kwargs)
+
+    for data in [doc, {'_default': {}}, {'b': {'1': {'text': 'こんにちは' * 100}}, 'a': {}}]:
+        storage.write(data)
+
+        # The file contains exactly the new data ...
+        expected = json.dumps(data, **kwargs).encode('utf-8')
+        assert tmpdir.join('test.db').read_binary() == expected
+
+        # ... which is returned by subsequent reads ...
+        assert storage.read() == data
+
+        # ... and no temporary files are left behind
+        assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    storage.close()
+
+    with TinyDB(path, encoding='utf-8') as db:
+        assert db.table('b').all() == [{'text': 'こんにちは' * 100}]
+
+
+def test_json_write_failure_keeps_contents(tmpdir):
+    resource = pytest.importorskip('resource')
+
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    db = TinyDB(path)
+    db.insert({'int': 1})
+    before = tmpdir.join('test.db').read_binary()
+
+    # Make writing a larger database state fail halfway by limiting the size
+    # of written files. Like CPython does by default, ignore SIGXFSZ so that
+    # exceeding the limit raises an error instead of killing the process.
+    soft_limit, hard_limit = resource.getrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE)
+    sigxfsz_handler = signal.signal(signal.SIGXFSZ, signal.SIG_IGN)
+    resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE, (len(before) + 100, hard_limit))
+    try:
+        with pytest.raises(OSError):
+            db.insert({'text': 'x' * 100000})
+    finally:
+        resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE, (soft_limit, hard_limit))
+        signal.signal(signal.SIGXFSZ, sigxfsz_handler)
+
+    # The database file is unchanged and no temporary file is left behind
+    assert tmpdir.join('test.db').read_binary() == before
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    # The database can still be used
+    assert db.all() == [{'int': 1}]
+    db.insert({'int': 2})
+    assert db.all() == [{'int': 1}, {'int': 2}]
+    db.close()
+
+
+def test_json_replace_failure_keeps_contents(tmpdir, monkeypatch):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    db = TinyDB(path)
+    db.insert({'int': 1})
+    before = tmpdir.join('test.db').read_binary()
+
+    def fail(src, dst):
+        raise OSError('Replacing failed')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as patch:
+        patch.setattr(os, 'replace', fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='Replacing failed'):
+            db.insert({'int': 2})
+
+    assert tmpdir.join('test.db').read_binary() == before
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    assert db.all() == [{'int': 1}]
+    db.insert({'int': 3})
+    assert db.all() == [{'int': 1}, {'int': 3}]
+    db.close()
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(sys.platform == 'win32', reason="Windows can't replace open files")
+def test_json_read_writes_of_other_instance(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    db1 = TinyDB(path)
+    db2 = TinyDB(path)
+
+    db1.insert({'int': 1})
+    assert db2.all() == [{'int': 1}]
+
+    db2.insert({'int': 2})
+    assert db1.all() == [{'int': 1}, {'int': 2}]
+
+    db1.close()
+    db2.close()
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(sys.platform == 'win32', reason='Requires POSIX file permissions')
+def test_json_write_keeps_permissions(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    db = TinyDB(path)
+    os.chmod(path, 0o640)
+
+    db.insert({'int': 1})
+
+    assert stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(path).st_mode) == 0o640
+    db.close()
+
+
+def test_json_write_through_symlink(tmpdir):
+    target = tmpdir.mkdir('data').join('test.db')
+    target.write('')
+    link = tmpdir.join('link.db')
+    try:
+        os.symlink(str(target), str(link))
+    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
+        pytest.skip('Symlinks are not supported')
+
+    db = TinyDB(str(link))
+    db.insert({'int': 1})
+
+    # The symlink is kept and the file it points to is updated
+    assert os.path.islink(str(link))
+    assert json.loads(target.read()) == {'_default': {'1': {'int': 1}}}
+    assert sorted(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == ['data', 'link.db']
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir.join('data'))) == ['test.db']
+    db.close()

+    Make changes to the entries of a directory (like a file having been
+    replaced) durable by syncing the directory itself.
+
+    This is done on a best-effort basis as not all platforms (e.g. Windows)
+    and file systems support opening or syncing directories.
+
+    :param path: The directory to sync.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+

+        # Writing the database file in place could leave it partially written
+        # if writing fails halfway (e.g. when the disk is full). Instead, the
+        # data is written to a temporary file in the same directory which then
+        # atomically replaces the database file. This way the database file

+        fd, temp_path = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix='.' + filename + '.', suffix='.tmp', dir=directory)

+                temp_file.write(serialized)

+                temp_file.flush()
+                os.fsync(temp_file.fileno())

+            # The temporary file is only accessible by its owner, so give it
+            # the permissions of the database file
+            try:

+            except OSError:

+            # to the old file is closed first
+            self._handle.close()
+            os.replace(temp_path, self._path)
+        except BaseException:

+            # contains the previous data.
+            try:
+                os.remove(temp_path)
+            except OSError:
+                pass
+
+            raise
+        finally:

+            # The file now contains the data just written (or still the
+            # previous data if replacing it failed).
+            if self._handle.closed:
+                self._handle = open(self._path, mode=self._reopen_mode, encoding=self._encoding)

+        _fsync_directory(directory)

+            return

+def test_json_write_failure_keeps_file(tmp_path):
+    resource = pytest.importorskip('resource')
+
+    path = tmp_path / 'test.db'
+    db = TinyDB(path)
+    db.insert({'name': 'old'})
+    before = path.read_bytes()
+
+    # Make the operating system refuse to write more than a few hundred
+    # bytes, so writing the new state fails half-way through
+    soft, hard = resource.getrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE)
+    resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE, (len(before) + 256, hard))
+    try:
+        with pytest.raises(OSError):
+            db.insert({'name': 'x' * 100000})
+    finally:
+        resource.setrlimit(resource.RLIMIT_FSIZE, (soft, hard))
+
+    # The file still holds exactly the old (valid) contents ...
+    assert path.read_bytes() == before
+    assert json.loads(before) == {'_default': {'1': {'name': 'old'}}}
+    # ... no staging file has been left behind ...
+    assert os.listdir(tmp_path) == ['test.db']
+    # ... and the database keeps working
+    assert db.all() == [{'name': 'old'}]
+    db.insert({'name': 'new'})
+    assert [doc['name'] for doc in db.all()] == ['old', 'new']
+    db.close()
+

+def _owner_only(path: str, flags: int) -> int:
+    """
+    Opener for :func:`open` that creates files only their owner can access.
+
+    Used for staging files, so their contents are never more exposed than
+    the database file's, even before :func:`_copy_permissions` ran.
+    """
+    return os.open(path, flags, 0o600)
+
+
+def _copy_permissions(source: str, destination: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Give ``destination`` the permission bits of ``source``.
+
+    This is best effort: if ``source`` is gone or the file system does not
+    support permissions, ``destination`` keeps the ones it was created with.
+    """
+    try:
+        os.chmod(destination, stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(source).st_mode))
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+
+
+def _sync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to the entries of the directory ``path`` (e.g. a rename)
+    to disk.
+
+    This is best effort: not every platform allows opening directories
+    (Windows doesn't) and the rename itself has already taken place anyway.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+
+def _discard(handle: IO, path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Close ``handle`` and delete the file at ``path``, ignoring all errors
+    (like the ones that are the reason for discarding the file).
+    """
+    try:
+        handle.close()
+    except Exception:
+        pass
+
+    try:
+        os.remove(path)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+
+

+.. note::
+    ``JSONStorage`` writes atomically: it writes the data to a temporary file
+    in the same directory and then replaces the database file with it. So the
+    database file always contains either the old or the new data, even if the
+    process dies or the disk runs full while writing. The temporary file is
+    named ``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``. This requires the directory

+    open, so writing fails in that case.

+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        old_contents = handle.read()
+    old_stat = os.stat(path)
+
+    def fail(*args):
+        raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, 'No space left on device')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
+        m.setattr(os, failing_function, fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='No space left on device'):
+            storage.write(new_data)
+
+    # The database file is exactly as it was...
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        assert handle.read() == old_contents
+    assert os.path.samestat(os.stat(path), old_stat)
+    assert os.stat(path).st_mtime_ns == old_stat.st_mtime_ns
+    assert storage.read() == old_data
+
+    # ...and the storage can still be used
+    storage.write(new_data)
+    assert storage.read() == new_data
+    storage.close()
+

+@pytest.mark.parametrize('encoding, kwargs', [
+    ('utf-8', {'indent': 4, 'sort_keys': True}),
+    ('utf-16', {'ensure_ascii': False}),
+    ('cp936', {'ensure_ascii': False, 'indent': 2, 'separators': (',', ': ')}),
+])
+def test_json_read_after_write(tmpdir, encoding, kwargs):
+    """
+    Reads see what has just been written, the file contains exactly what
+    ``json.dumps`` returns for the given options (in the given encoding) and
+    no temporary files are left behind
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path, encoding=encoding, **kwargs)
+
+    for i in [3, 10, 0, 7, 1]:
+        data = {'_default': {str(n): {'text': u'こんにちは世界', 'n': n}
+                             for n in range(i)}}
+        storage.write(data)
+
+        assert storage.read() == data
+
+        with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+            serialized = json.dumps(data, **kwargs)
+            expected = serialized.replace('\n', os.linesep).encode(encoding)
+            assert handle.read() == expected
+
+        assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    storage.close()

+def test_json_read_after_failed_reopen(tmpdir, monkeypatch):
+    """
+    If re-opening the file after replacing it fails, the write has still
+    succeeded and the next read re-opens the file
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    open_for_reading = storage._open_for_reading
+    calls = []
+
+    def fail_once():
+        calls.append(None)
+        if len(calls) == 1:
+            raise OSError(errno.EMFILE, 'Too many open files')
+        return open_for_reading()
+
+    monkeypatch.setattr(storage, '_open_for_reading', fail_once)
+
+    storage.write(doc)
+    assert storage.read() == doc
+    assert len(calls) == 2
+    storage.close()
+

+def test_json_crash_during_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir):
+    """
+    If the process dies in the middle of a write, the database file has to
+    keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    script = textwrap.dedent('''
+        import os, sys
+        from tinydb.storages import JSONStorage
+
+        storage = JSONStorage(sys.argv[1])
+        storage.write({'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}})
+
+        # Kill the process after the new data has been written, but before
+        # the write has finished
+        os.fsync = lambda fd: os._exit(23)
+        storage.write({'_default': {}})
+    ''')
+
+    # Make sure the subprocess imports the same TinyDB as this test
+    env = dict(os.environ)
+    tinydb_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(tinydb.__file__))
+    env['PYTHONPATH'] = os.pathsep.join(
+        filter(None, [tinydb_root, env.get('PYTHONPATH')])
+    )
+
+    process = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-c', script, path], env=env,
+                             timeout=60)
+    assert process.returncode == 23
+
+    with open(path) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == {'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}}
+

+def test_json_write_through_symlink(tmpdir):
+    target = str(tmpdir.join('target.db'))
+    link = str(tmpdir.join('link.db'))
+    touch(target, create_dirs=False)
+

+        os.symlink(target, link)
+    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
+        pytest.skip('Symlinks are not supported')
+

+    storage.write(doc)
+    storage.close()
+

+    assert os.path.islink(link)
+    with open(target) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == doc
+

+def test_json_relative_path_after_chdir(tmpdir, monkeypatch):
+    monkeypatch.chdir(tmpdir)
+    storage = JSONStorage('test.db')
+
+    # Writes go to the file that was opened, not to a file relative to the
+    # new working directory
+    monkeypatch.chdir(tmpdir.mkdir('other'))
+    storage.write(doc)
+    storage.close()
+
+    assert json.loads(tmpdir.join('test.db').read()) == doc
+    assert tmpdir.join('other').listdir() == []
+

+@pytest.mark.skipif(os.name == 'nt',
+                    reason="Windows can't replace files that are open")
+def test_json_storages_sharing_a_file(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    first = JSONStorage(path)
+    second = JSONStorage(path)
+
+    # Each storage sees what the other one has written
+    first.write({'_default': {'1': {'a': 1}}})
+    assert second.read() == {'_default': {'1': {'a': 1}}}
+
+    second.write({'_default': {'2': {'b': 2}}})
+    assert first.read() == {'_default': {'2': {'b': 2}}}
+
+    first.close()
+    second.close()
+

+def _fsync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to a directory's entries (like a renamed file) to disk.
+
+    This is best-effort: it's not supported everywhere (e.g. directories
+    can't be opened on Windows) and it's only called after a file has
+    already been replaced, so failing here would wrongly report the write
+    itself as failed.
+
+    :param path: The directory to flush.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+

     The abstract base class for all Storages.
@@ -79,8 +104,16 @@ class Storage(ABC):
 class JSONStorage(Storage):
     """
     Store the data in a JSON file.
+
+    Writes are atomic: the data is first written to a temporary file in the
+    same directory, which then replaces the database file. So the database
+    file always contains either the old or the new data, even if a write is
+    interrupted (e.g. because the process is killed or the disk is full).
     """
 
+    #: The temporary files are named ``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``
+    _TEMP_SUFFIX = '.tinydb.tmp'
+

+        # Instead of overwriting the database file in place, write the data
+        # to a temporary file and then move it into place. Moving a file is
+        # atomic, so the database file never ends up half-written, even if
+        # the process dies or the disk runs full while writing. The temporary
+        # file has to be in the same directory, as files can only be moved
+        # atomically within the same file system.
+        directory = os.path.dirname(self._path)
+        with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(

+            prefix=self._temp_prefix, suffix=self._TEMP_SUFFIX, delete=False
+        ) as temp:

+

+            # replaces the database file

+        # first (it's re-opened below)
+        self._handle.close()
+
+        try:
+            os.replace(temp.name, self._path)
+        except BaseException:
+            # The database file hasn't been replaced, so don't leave the
+            # temporary file behind
+            try:
+                os.remove(temp.name)
+            except OSError:
+                pass
+

+            # read tries again, so a successful write isn't reported as
+            # failed and the storage keeps working.
+            if self._handle.closed:
+                try:
+                    self._handle = self._open_for_reading()
+                except OSError:
+                    pass
+

+        _fsync_directory(directory)

+    def _refresh_handle(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Make sure the handle reads the current version of the database file.
+
+        Writes replace the file, so the handle has to be re-opened when another
+        storage instance has written to the file (our handle would keep
+        reading the old version otherwise) or when re-opening it after a write
+        has failed.
+        """
+        if not self._handle.closed:
+            try:
+                current = os.stat(self._path)
+            except OSError:
+                # The file has been removed, so keep reading what we've got
+                return
+
+            if os.path.samestat(os.fstat(self._handle.fileno()), current):
+                return

     user-controlled values for callable arguments (e.g. ``default`` or
     ``cls``) as they are executed in-process on every write operation.
 
+.. note::
+    ``JSONStorage`` writes atomically: it writes the data to a temporary file
+    in the same directory and then replaces the database file with it. So the
+    database file always contains either the old or the new data, even if the
+    process dies or the disk runs full while writing. If a write fails, the
+    temporary file is removed right away. If the process is killed while
+    writing, its temporary file (``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``) is
+    removed the next time the database is opened for writing. This requires
+    the directory containing the database file to be writable. On Windows, a
+    file can't be replaced while another program or ``TinyDB`` instance has it
+    open, so writing fails in that case.
+
 To modify the default storage for all ``TinyDB`` instances, set the
 ``default_storage_class`` class variable:

+@pytest.mark.parametrize('failing_function', ['fsync', 'replace'])
+def test_json_failed_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir, monkeypatch,
+                                          failing_function):
+    """
+    If a write fails midway (e.g. because the disk is full), the database
+    file has to keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    old_data = {'_default': {str(i): {'value': 'x' * 50} for i in range(20)}}
+    new_data = {'_default': {}}
+

+    storage.write(old_data)
+
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        old_contents = handle.read()
+    old_stat = os.stat(path)
+
+    def fail(*args):
+        raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, 'No space left on device')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
+        m.setattr(os, failing_function, fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='No space left on device'):
+            storage.write(new_data)

+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+

+def test_json_removes_temp_files_of_interrupted_writes(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    touch(path, create_dirs=False)
+
+    leftover = tmpdir.join('.test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp')
+    leftover.write('{"_default": {"1": ')
+
+    # Files that only look similar are kept, like the temporary files of the
+    # database "test.db.v2"
+    unrelated = ['.test.db.backup', '.test.db.v2.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp',
+                 'test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp', 'other.json']
+    for name in unrelated:
+        tmpdir.join(name).write('')
+
+    # Opening the database read-only doesn't remove anything
+    JSONStorage(path, access_mode='r').close()
+    assert leftover.exists()
+
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert not leftover.exists()
+    assert sorted(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == sorted(unrelated + ['test.db'])
+

+def _fsync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to a directory's entries (like a renamed file) to disk.
+
+    This is best-effort: it's not supported everywhere (e.g. directories
+    can't be opened on Windows) and it's only called after a file has
+    already been replaced, so failing here would wrongly report the write
+    itself as failed.
+
+    :param path: The directory to flush.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+

     """
     Store the data in a JSON file.
+
+    Writes are atomic: the data is first written to a temporary file in the
+    same directory, which then replaces the database file. So the database
+    file always contains either the old or the new data, even if a write is
+    interrupted (e.g. because the process is killed or the disk is full).
     """

+        # atomic, so the database file never ends up half-written, even if

+                # (JSON parsers ignore trailing whitespace)
+                temp.write('\n')
+
+                # Ensure the data has been written to disk before the file
+                # replaces the database file
+                temp.flush()
+                os.fsync(temp.fileno())
+
+            # Temporary files are only accessible by the current user, so
+            # keep the permissions of the database file (if possible)
+            try:
+                mode = stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(self._path).st_mode)
+                os.chmod(temp_path, mode)
+            except OSError:
+                pass

+            # first (it's re-opened below)
+            self._handle.close()

+        except BaseException:

+            try:
+                os.remove(temp_path)
+            except OSError:

+            # read tries again, so a successful write isn't reported as
+            # failed and the storage keeps working.
+            if self._handle.closed:
+                try:
+                    self._handle = self._open_for_reading()
+                except OSError:

+        _fsync_directory(directory)

+    def _refresh_handle(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Make sure the handle reads the current version of the database file.
+
+        Writes replace the file, so the handle has to be re-opened when another
+        storage instance has written to the file (our handle would keep
+        reading the old version otherwise) or when re-opening it after a write
+        has failed.
+        """
+        if not self._handle.closed:
+            try:

+            except OSError:
+                # The file has been removed, so keep reading what we've got
+                return

+    def _open_for_reading(self) -> IO[Any]:
+        return open(self._path, mode=self._read_mode, encoding=self._encoding)
+
+    def _remove_temp_files(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Remove temporary files left behind by interrupted writes.
+        """
+        # Only match names ``write`` uses for this file. Their random part
+        # never contains dots, which rules out temporary files of other
+        # databases whose names start with this one's (like ``db.json.v2``).
+        temp_file = re.compile(
+            re.escape(self._temp_prefix) + r'[^.]+' +
+            re.escape(self._TEMP_SUFFIX)
+        )
+        directory = os.path.dirname(self._path)
+

+            names = os.listdir(directory)
+        except OSError:
+            return
+
+        for name in names:
+            if temp_file.fullmatch(name):
+                try:
+                    os.remove(os.path.join(directory, name))
+                except OSError:
+                    pass

     user-controlled values for callable arguments (e.g. ``default`` or

+.. note::
+    ``JSONStorage`` writes atomically: it writes the data to a temporary file
+    in the same directory and then replaces the database file with it. So the
+    database file always contains either the old or the new data, even if the
+    process dies or the disk runs full while writing. If a write fails, the
+    temporary file is removed right away. If the process is killed while
+    writing, its temporary file (``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``) is
+    removed the next time the database is opened for writing. This requires
+    the directory containing the database file to be writable. On Windows, a
+    file can't be replaced while another program or ``TinyDB`` instance has it
+    open, so writing fails in that case.
+

 ``default_storage_class`` class variable:

+@pytest.mark.parametrize('failing_function', ['fsync', 'replace'])
+def test_json_failed_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir, monkeypatch,
+                                          failing_function):
+    """
+    If a write fails midway (e.g. because the disk is full), the database
+    file has to keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    old_data = {'_default': {str(i): {'value': 'x' * 50} for i in range(20)}}
+    new_data = {'_default': {}}
+
+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    storage.write(old_data)
+
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        old_contents = handle.read()
+    old_stat = os.stat(path)
+
+    def fail(*args):
+        raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, 'No space left on device')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
+        m.setattr(os, failing_function, fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='No space left on device'):
+            storage.write(new_data)
+
+    # The database file is exactly as it was...
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        assert handle.read() == old_contents
+    assert os.path.samestat(os.stat(path), old_stat)
+    assert os.stat(path).st_mtime_ns == old_stat.st_mtime_ns
+    assert storage.read() == old_data
+
+    # ...no temporary file has been left behind...
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    # ...and the storage can still be used
+    storage.write(new_data)
+    assert storage.read() == new_data
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+    storage.close()
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize('encoding, kwargs', [
+    ('utf-8', {'indent': 4, 'sort_keys': True}),
+    ('utf-8', {'ensure_ascii': False}),
+    ('utf-8', {'ensure_ascii': False, 'indent': 2, 'separators': (',', ': ')}),
+])
+def test_json_read_after_write(tmpdir, encoding, kwargs):
+    """
+    Reads see what has just been written, the file contains exactly what
+    ``json.dumps`` returns for the given options (in the given encoding) and
+    no temporary files are left behind
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path, encoding=encoding, **kwargs)
+
+    for i in [3, 10, 0, 7, 1]:
+        data = {'_default': {str(n): {'text': u'こんにちは世界', 'n': n}
+                             for n in range(i)}}
+        storage.write(data)
+
+        assert storage.read() == data
+
+        with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+            serialized = json.dumps(data, **kwargs)
+            expected = serialized.replace('\n', os.linesep).encode(encoding)
+            assert handle.read() == expected
+
+        assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    storage.close()
+

+def test_json_read_after_failed_reopen(tmpdir, monkeypatch):
+    """
+    If re-opening the file after replacing it fails, the write has still
+    succeeded and the next read re-opens the file
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    open_for_reading = storage._open_for_reading
+    calls = []
+
+    def fail_once():
+        calls.append(None)
+        if len(calls) == 1:
+            raise OSError(errno.EMFILE, 'Too many open files')
+        return open_for_reading()
+
+    monkeypatch.setattr(storage, '_open_for_reading', fail_once)
+
+    storage.write(doc)
+    assert storage.read() == doc
+    assert len(calls) == 2
+    storage.close()
+

+def test_json_crash_during_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir):
+    """
+    If the process dies in the middle of a write, the database file has to
+    keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    script = textwrap.dedent('''
+        import os, sys
+        from tinydb.storages import JSONStorage
+
+        storage = JSONStorage(sys.argv[1])
+        storage.write({'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}})
+
+        # Kill the process after the new data has been written, but before
+        # the write has finished
+        os.fsync = lambda fd: os._exit(23)
+        storage.write({'_default': {}})
+    ''')
+
+    # Make sure the subprocess imports the same TinyDB as this test
+    env = dict(os.environ)
+    tinydb_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(tinydb.__file__))
+    env['PYTHONPATH'] = os.pathsep.join(
+        filter(None, [tinydb_root, env.get('PYTHONPATH')])
+    )
+
+    process = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-c', script, path], env=env,
+                             timeout=60)
+    assert process.returncode == 23
+
+    with open(path) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == {'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}}
+
+    # The killed process couldn't remove its temporary file, so it's removed
+    # when the database is opened for writing again
+    assert len(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == 2
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+
+def test_json_removes_temp_files_of_interrupted_writes(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    touch(path, create_dirs=False)
+
+    leftover = tmpdir.join('.test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp')
+    leftover.write('{"_default": {"1": ')
+
+    # Files that only look similar are kept, like the temporary files of the
+    # database "test.db.v2"
+    unrelated = ['.test.db.backup', '.test.db.v2.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp',
+                 'test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp', 'other.json']
+    for name in unrelated:
+        tmpdir.join(name).write('')
+
+    # Opening the database read-only doesn't remove anything
+    JSONStorage(path, access_mode='r').close()
+    assert leftover.exists()
+
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert not leftover.exists()
+    assert sorted(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == sorted(unrelated + ['test.db'])
+
+
+@pytest.mark.skipif(os.name != 'posix', reason='Needs POSIX permissions')

+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    os.chmod(path, 0o640)
+
+    storage.write(doc)
+    storage.close()
+
+    assert stat.S_IMODE(os.stat(path).st_mode) == 0o640

+    storage.close()

+    # The symlink is kept and the file it points to is updated
+    assert os.path.islink(link)
+    with open(target) as handle:

+        # never contains dots, which rules out temporary files of other
+        # databases whose names start with this one's (like ``db.json.v2``).

+                except OSError:
+                    pass

@@ -643,6 +643,18 @@ To use the in-memory storage, use:
     user-controlled values for callable arguments (e.g. ``default`` or
     ``cls``) as they are executed in-process on every write operation.
 
+.. note::
+    ``JSONStorage`` writes atomically: it writes the data to a temporary file
+    in the same directory and then replaces the database file with it. So the
+    database file always contains either the old or the new data, even if the
+    process dies or the disk runs full while writing. If a write fails, the
+    temporary file is removed right away. If the process is killed while
+    writing, its temporary file (``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``) is
+    removed the next time the database is opened for writing. This requires
+    the directory containing the database file to be writable. On Windows, a
+    file can't be replaced while another program or ``TinyDB`` instance has it
+    open, so writing fails in that case.
+
 To modify the default storage for all ``TinyDB`` instances, set the
 ``default_storage_class`` class variable:

+@pytest.mark.parametrize('failing_function', ['fsync', 'replace'])
+def test_json_failed_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir, monkeypatch,
+                                          failing_function):
+    """
+    If a write fails midway (e.g. because the disk is full), the database
+    file has to keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    old_data = {'_default': {str(i): {'value': 'x' * 50} for i in range(20)}}
+    new_data = {'_default': {}}
+
+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    storage.write(old_data)
+
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        old_contents = handle.read()
+    old_stat = os.stat(path)
+
+    def fail(*args):
+        raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, 'No space left on device')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
+        m.setattr(os, failing_function, fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='No space left on device'):
+            storage.write(new_data)
+
+    # The database file is exactly as it was...
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        assert handle.read() == old_contents
+    assert os.path.samestat(os.stat(path), old_stat)
+    assert os.stat(path).st_mtime_ns == old_stat.st_mtime_ns
+    assert storage.read() == old_data
+
+    # ...no temporary file has been left behind...
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    # ...and the storage can still be used
+    storage.write(new_data)
+    assert storage.read() == new_data
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+    storage.close()
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize('encoding, kwargs', [
+    ('utf-8', {'indent': 4, 'sort_keys': True}),
+    ('utf-16', {'ensure_ascii': False}),
+    ('cp936', {'ensure_ascii': False, 'indent': 2, 'separators': (',', ': ')}),
+])
+def test_json_read_after_write(tmpdir, encoding, kwargs):
+    """
+    Reads see what has just been written, the file contains exactly what
+    ``json.dumps`` returns for the given options (in the given encoding) and
+    no temporary files are left behind
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path, encoding=encoding, **kwargs)
+
+    for i in [3, 10, 0, 7, 1]:
+        data = {'_default': {str(n): {'text': u'こんにちは世界', 'n': n}
+                             for n in range(i)}}
+        storage.write(data)
+
+        assert storage.read() == data
+
+        with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+            serialized = json.dumps(data, **kwargs)
+            expected = serialized.replace('\n', os.linesep).encode(encoding)
+            assert handle.read() == expected
+
+        assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    storage.close()
+

+def test_json_crash_during_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir):
+    """
+    If the process dies in the middle of a write, the database file has to
+    keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    script = textwrap.dedent('''
+        import os, sys
+        from tinydb.storages import JSONStorage
+
+        storage = JSONStorage(sys.argv[1])
+        storage.write({'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}})
+
+        # Kill the process after the new data has been written, but before
+        # the write has finished
+        os.fsync = lambda fd: os._exit(23)
+        storage.write({'_default': {}})
+    ''')
+
+    # Make sure the subprocess imports the same TinyDB as this test
+    env = dict(os.environ)
+    tinydb_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(tinydb.__file__))
+    env['PYTHONPATH'] = os.pathsep.join(
+        filter(None, [tinydb_root, env.get('PYTHONPATH')])
+    )
+
+    process = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-c', script, path], env=env,
+                             timeout=60)
+    assert process.returncode == 23
+
+    with open(path) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == {'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}}
+
+    # The killed process couldn't remove its temporary file, so it's removed
+    # when the database is opened for writing again
+    assert len(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == 2
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+
+def test_json_removes_temp_files_of_interrupted_writes(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    touch(path, create_dirs=False)
+
+    leftover = tmpdir.join('.test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp')
+    leftover.write('{"_default": {"1": ')
+
+    # Files that only look similar are kept, like the temporary files of the
+    # database "test.db.v2"
+    unrelated = ['.test.db.backup', '.test.db.v2.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp',
+                 'test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp', 'other.json']
+    for name in unrelated:
+        tmpdir.join(name).write('')
+
+    # Opening the database read-only doesn't remove anything
+    JSONStorage(path, access_mode='r').close()
+    assert leftover.exists()
+
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert not leftover.exists()
+    assert sorted(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == sorted(unrelated + ['test.db'])
+

+def test_json_write_through_symlink(tmpdir):

+

+    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
+        pytest.skip('Symlinks are not supported')
+
+    storage = JSONStorage(link)
+    storage.write(doc)
+    storage.close()
+
+    # The symlink is kept and the file it points to is updated
+    assert os.path.islink(link)
+    with open(target) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == doc

+def _fsync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to a directory's entries (like a renamed file) to disk.
+
+    This is best-effort: it's not supported everywhere (e.g. directories
+    can't be opened on Windows) and it's only called after a file has
+    already been replaced, so failing here would wrongly report the write
+    itself as failed.
+
+    :param path: The directory to flush.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+

+    def _remove_temp_files(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Remove temporary files left behind by interrupted writes.
+        """
+        # Only match names ``write`` uses for this file. Their random part
+        # never contains dots, which rules out temporary files of other
+        # databases whose names start with this one's (like ``db.json.v2``).
+        temp_file = re.compile(
+            re.escape(self._temp_prefix) + r'[^.]+' +
+            re.escape(self._TEMP_SUFFIX)
+        )
+        directory = os.path.dirname(self._target())
+
+        try:
+            names = os.listdir(directory)
+        except OSError:
+            return
+
+        for name in names:
+            if temp_file.fullmatch(name):
+                try:
+                    os.remove(os.path.join(directory, name))
+                except OSError:
+                    pass

+.. note::
+    ``JSONStorage`` writes atomically: it writes the data to a temporary file
+    in the same directory and then replaces the database file with it. So the
+    database file always contains either the old or the new data, even if the
+    process dies or the disk runs full while writing. If a write fails, the
+    temporary file is removed right away. If the process is killed while
+    writing, its temporary file (``.<file name>.<random>.tinydb.tmp``) is
+    removed the next time the database is opened for writing. This requires
+    the directory containing the database file to be writable. On Windows, a
+    file can't be replaced while another program or ``TinyDB`` instance has it
+    open, so writing fails in that case.
+

+@pytest.mark.parametrize('failing_function', ['fsync', 'replace'])
+def test_json_failed_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir, monkeypatch,
+                                          failing_function):
+    """
+    If a write fails midway (e.g. because the disk is full), the database
+    file has to keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    old_data = {'_default': {str(i): {'value': 'x' * 50} for i in range(20)}}
+    new_data = {'_default': {}}
+
+    storage = JSONStorage(path)
+    storage.write(old_data)
+
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        old_contents = handle.read()
+    old_stat = os.stat(path)
+
+    def fail(*args):
+        raise OSError(errno.ENOSPC, 'No space left on device')
+
+    with monkeypatch.context() as m:
+        m.setattr(os, failing_function, fail)
+
+        with pytest.raises(OSError, match='No space left on device'):
+            storage.write(new_data)
+
+    # The database file is exactly as it was...
+    with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+        assert handle.read() == old_contents
+    assert os.path.samestat(os.stat(path), old_stat)
+    assert os.stat(path).st_mtime_ns == old_stat.st_mtime_ns
+    assert storage.read() == old_data
+
+    # ...no temporary file has been left behind...
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    # ...and the storage can still be used
+    storage.write(new_data)
+    assert storage.read() == new_data
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+    storage.close()
+
+
+@pytest.mark.parametrize('encoding, kwargs', [
+    ('utf-8', {'indent': 4, 'sort_keys': True, 'ensure_ascii': False}),
+    ('utf-16', {'ensure_ascii': False}),
+    ('cp936', {'ensure_ascii': False, 'indent': 2, 'separators': (',', ': ')}),
+])
+def test_json_read_after_write(tmpdir, encoding, kwargs):
+    """
+    Reads see what has just been written, the file contains exactly what
+    ``json.dumps`` returns for the given options (in the given encoding) and
+    no temporary files are left behind
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    storage = JSONStorage(path, encoding=encoding, **kwargs)
+
+    for i in [3, 10, 0, 7, 1]:
+        data = {'_default': {str(n): {'text': u'こんにちは世界', 'n': n}
+                             for n in range(i)}}
+        storage.write(data)
+
+        assert storage.read() == data
+
+        with open(path, 'rb') as handle:
+            serialized = json.dumps(data, **kwargs)
+            expected = serialized.replace('\n', os.linesep).encode(encoding)
+            assert handle.read() == expected
+
+        assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+
+    storage.close()
+

+def test_json_crash_during_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir):
+    """
+    If the process dies in the middle of a write, the database file has to
+    keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    script = textwrap.dedent('''
+        import os, sys
+        from tinydb.storages import JSONStorage
+
+        storage = JSONStorage(sys.argv[1])
+        storage.write({'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}})
+
+        # Kill the process after the new data has been written, but before
+        # the write has finished
+        os.fsync = lambda fd: os._exit(23)
+        storage.write({'_default': {}})
+    ''')
+
+    # Make sure the subprocess imports the same TinyDB as this test
+    env = dict(os.environ)
+    tinydb_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(tinydb.__file__))
+    env['PYTHONPATH'] = os.pathsep.join(
+        filter(None, [tinydb_root, env.get('PYTHONPATH')])
+    )
+
+    process = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-c', script, path], env=env,
+                             timeout=60)
+    assert process.returncode == 23
+
+    with open(path) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == {'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}}
+
+    # The killed process couldn't remove its temporary file, so it's removed
+    # when the database is opened for writing again
+    assert len(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == 2
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert os.listdir(str(tmpdir)) == ['test.db']
+

+def test_json_removes_temp_files_of_interrupted_writes(tmpdir):
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    touch(path, create_dirs=False)
+
+    leftover = tmpdir.join('.test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp')
+    leftover.write('{"_default": {"1": ')
+
+    # Files that only look similar are kept, like the temporary files of the
+    # database "test.db.v2"
+    unrelated = ['.test.db.backup', '.test.db.v2.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp',
+                 'test.db.k3j2_9ab.tinydb.tmp', 'other.json']
+    for name in unrelated:
+        tmpdir.join(name).write('')
+
+    # Opening the database read-only doesn't remove anything
+    JSONStorage(path, access_mode='r').close()
+    assert leftover.exists()
+
+    JSONStorage(path).close()
+    assert not leftover.exists()
+    assert sorted(os.listdir(str(tmpdir))) == sorted(unrelated + ['test.db'])

+def _fsync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to a directory's entries (like a renamed file) to disk.
+
+    This is best-effort: it's not supported everywhere (e.g. directories
+    can't be opened on Windows) and it's only called after a file has
+    already been replaced, so failing here would wrongly report the write
+    itself as failed.
+
+    :param path: The directory to flush.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+

+    """

+    file has to keep its previous contents

+    storage.write(new_data)
+    assert storage.read() == new_data
+    storage.close()

+def test_json_crash_during_write_keeps_old_data(tmpdir):
+    """
+    If the process dies in the middle of a write, the database file has to
+    keep its previous contents
+    """
+    path = str(tmpdir.join('test.db'))
+    script = textwrap.dedent('''

+        from tinydb.storages import JSONStorage
+
+        storage = JSONStorage(sys.argv[1])
+        storage.write({'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}})
+
+        # Kill the process after the new data has been written, but before
+        # the write has finished
+        os.fsync = lambda fd: os._exit(23)
+        storage.write({'_default': {}})
+    ''')
+
+    # Make sure the subprocess imports the same TinyDB as this test
+    env = dict(os.environ)
+    tinydb_root = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(tinydb.__file__))
+    env['PYTHONPATH'] = os.pathsep.join(
+        filter(None, [tinydb_root, env.get('PYTHONPATH')])
+    )
+
+    process = subprocess.run([sys.executable, '-c', script, path], env=env,
+                             timeout=60)
+    assert process.returncode == 23
+
+    with open(path) as handle:
+        assert json.load(handle) == {'_default': {'1': {'value': 'old' * 100}}}

+def _fsync_directory(path: str) -> None:
+    """
+    Flush changes to a directory's entries (like a renamed file) to disk.
+
+    This is best-effort: it's not supported everywhere (e.g. directories
+    can't be opened on Windows) and it's only called after a file has
+    already been replaced, so failing here would wrongly report the write
+    itself as failed.
+
+    :param path: The directory to flush.
+    """
+    try:
+        fd = os.open(path, os.O_RDONLY)
+    except OSError:
+        return
+
+    try:
+        os.fsync(fd)
+    except OSError:
+        pass
+    finally:
+        os.close(fd)
+
+

@@ -79,6 +104,11 @@ class Storage(ABC):
 class JSONStorage(Storage):
     """
     Store the data in a JSON file.
+
+    Writes are atomic: the data is first written to a temporary file in the
+    same directory, which then replaces the database file. So the database
+    file always contains either the old or the new data, even if a write is
+    interrupted (e.g. because the process is killed or the disk is full).
     """
 
     def __init__(self, path: str, create_dirs=False, encoding=None, access_mode='r+', **kwargs):

+        # Open the file. The handle is only used for reading, as writes
+        # replace the whole file (see ``write``).
         self._handle = open(path, mode=self._mode, encoding=encoding)
 
+        # Remember what's needed for writing and re-opening the file
+        self._encoding = encoding
+        self._writable = self._handle.writable()
+        self._read_mode = 'rb' if 'b' in self._mode else 'r'
+        self._write_mode = 'wb' if 'b' in self._mode else 'w'
+
+        # Resolve the path once, so writes keep going to the same file even if
+        # the working directory changes, and so they update the file a symlink
+        # points to instead of replacing the symlink itself
+        self._path = os.path.realpath(os.fsdecode(path))
+
     def close(self) -> None:
         self._handle.close()

+        # Make sure to read the current version of the file even if another
+        # storage instance has replaced it in the meantime
+        self._reopen_if_replaced()

         # its location
